```json
  "command": "cat > /tmp/pr_body_p4.md <<'EOF'\n## Week 6 — Phase 4: drawer → page restructures\n\nAdopts the prototype's dedicated-page model (ADR 0022 — **prototype wins on any drawer-vs-page conflict**). Every create/edit flow becomes a deep-linkable page; the **Share** drawer (and the read-only version-history drawer) are the only surviving drawers.\n\n### 4.1 — New Suite page (`1f437fa`)\n- Extracted a shared `SuiteForm` (name/description/connection picker + datasource-shaped run-target inputs) out of `SuiteDrawer`.\n- `/suites/new` reuses it; on create it continues to `/suites/:id/checks/new` so a fresh suite is never left empty.\n- `Suites.tsx` \"New suite\" → `navigate('/suites/new')`.\n\n### 4.2 — Edit flows → pages (`95c7cd2`) — dedups #204\n- `/connections/:id/edit` → `ConnectionEdit` (reuses `ConnectionForm`)\n- `/suites/:id/edit` → `SuiteEdit` (reuses `SuiteForm`)\n- `/suites/:id/checks/:checkId/edit` → `CheckEdit`\n- **Removed `ConnectionDrawer`, `SuiteDrawer`, `CheckDrawer`** + their tests; parents drop `editing`/`drawer` state for `navigate()`.\n- Create + edit share one form per entity (`ConnectionForm`/`SuiteForm` gained an edit mode; check form helpers were already shared). Added `getCheck`.\n- Version history stays the one read-only drawer (alongside Share).\n\n### 4.3 — New Connection source-select split (`94af936`)\n- Categorized **Select Source** step — Orchestration first, then Warehouses / Lakehouses / Cloud Storage, with an Orchestration lead-in nudge; cards gain the type avatar + a one-line blurb + a chevron → spec-driven **Configuration** step.\n- Grouping is presentation-only (`connectionSources.ts`); the load-bearing datasource-vs-orchestration split still flows through `CONNECTION_KIND` (CLAUDE.md §4).\n\n### Review fix (`ddbdc1d`)\n`/code-review` caught a real bug: the edit pages fetched via `useAsyncData` keyed on an internal nonce, so a param-only navigation between two edit URLs kept stale data and could PATCH the wrong id. Fixed by keying each edit view on its route id (remount → refetch + reseed; regression test added) and parallelized CheckEdit's `getConnection` fetch.\n\n### Verification\nLocal gates green: `tsc --noEmit` · ESLint (0 warnings) · Prettier · **259 vitest** · production build (recharts stays its own lazy chunk).\n\n### Deferred (noted, not filed)\nThe 3 edit pages share a load+header+Card shell that could fold into one `EditPageShell` wrapper — natural to do when Phase 6 adds the 4xx/5xx error branches.\n\nCloses part of #175 (Week 6 → 16/27).\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\ngh pr create --base main --head feature/w6-phase4-drawer-to-page \\\n  --title \"feat(frontend): Week 6 Phase 4 — drawer→page restructures (new-suite/edit pages + connection source-select)\" \\\n  --body-file /tmp/pr_body_p4.md 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Create the PR"
}
```

```
1	import { expect, test } from '@playwright/test';
2	
3	// Reads the seeded demo connections through the real API (proxy → api → DB).
4	// Names/labels come from backend/scripts/demo_data.py.
5	test.describe('Connections page', () => {
6	  test.beforeEach(async ({ page }) => {
7	    await page.goto('/connections');
8	    await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Connections', level: 3 })).toBeVisible();
9	  });
10	
11	  test('lists the seeded connections under kind sections, grouped by type', async ({ page }) => {
12	    // The two top-level kind sections (datasource vs orchestration) and the
13	    // per-type sub-headings are both present.
14	    await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Data sources', level: 4 })).toBeVisible();
15	    await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Orchestration', level: 4 })).toBeVisible();
16	    await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Snowflake', level: 5 })).toBeVisible();
17	
18	    // A datasource and an orchestration provider both render.
19	    await expect(page.getByText('snowflake-analytics').first()).toBeVisible();
20	    await expect(page.getByText('s3-datalake').first()).toBeVisible();
21	    await expect(page.getByText('airflow-dags').first()).toBeVisible();
22	  });
23	
24	  test('the edit drawer opens read-only on type + env (no create fields)', async ({ page }) => {
25	    // Open the ⋮ menu on a seeded Snowflake connection → Edit.
26	    const card = page.locator('.ant-card').filter({ hasText: 'snowflake-analytics' }).first();
27	    await card
28	      .locator('button')
29	      .filter({ has: page.locator('.anticon-more') })
30	      .click();
31	    await page.getByRole('menuitem', { name: 'Edit' }).click();
32	
33	    const drawer = page.getByRole('dialog', { name: 'Edit connection' });
34	    await expect(drawer).toBeVisible();
35	    // Type is shown read-only; name stays editable; the secret field is omitted
36	    // (rotation is the separate Re-auth flow) — i.e. none of the create-only UI.
37	    await expect(drawer.getByText('Snowflake')).toBeVisible();
38	    await expect(drawer.getByLabel('Name')).toBeVisible();
39	    await expect(drawer.getByLabel('Password')).toHaveCount(0);
40	    await drawer.getByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' }).click();
41	    await expect(drawer).toBeHidden();
42	  });
43	
44	  test('add a connection via the dedicated page, then delete it', async ({ page }) => {
45	    const name = `e2e-conn-${Date.now()}`;
46	
47	    await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Add connection' }).click();
48	    await expect(page).toHaveURL(/\/connections\/new$/);
49	
50	    // Step 1: the picker is split into the two kind sections; pick a datasource.
51	    await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Data sources', level: 5 })).toBeVisible();
52	    await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Orchestration', level: 5 })).toBeVisible();
53	    await page.getByText('Snowflake', { exact: true }).click();
54	
55	    // Step 2: the Snowflake form appears; fill the required fields + secret.
56	    await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: /New Snowflake connection/ })).toBeVisible();
57	    await page.getByLabel('Name').fill(name);
58	    const env = page.getByLabel('Environment');
59	    await env.click();
60	    await env.press('ArrowDown');
61	    await env.press('Enter');
62	    for (const label of ['Account', 'User', 'Database', 'Schema', 'Warehouse']) {
63	      await page.getByLabel(label, { exact: true }).fill(`${label.toLowerCase()}-val`);
64	    }
65	    await page.getByLabel('Password').fill('sekret');
66	    await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Create' }).click();
67	
68	    // Back on the list, the new connection card is visible.
69	    await expect(page).toHaveURL(/\/connections$/);
70	    const card = page.locator('.ant-card').filter({ hasText: name });
71	    await expect(card).toBeVisible();
72	
73	    // Clean up: the card's ⋮ menu → Delete → confirm.
74	    await card
75	      .locator('button')
76	      .filter({ has: page.locator('.anticon-more') })
77	      .click();
78	    await page.getByRole('menuitem', { name: 'Delete' }).click();
79	    await page
80	      .getByRole('dialog')
81	      .filter({ hasText: 'Delete' })
82	      .getByRole('button', { name: 'Delete' })
83	      .click();
84	    await expect(page.locator('.ant-card').filter({ hasText: name })).toHaveCount(0);

86	
87	  test('"Test all" kicks off a connectivity test on every connection', async ({ page }) => {
88	    const testAll = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Test all' });
89	    await expect(testAll).toBeEnabled();
90	    await testAll.click();
91	
92	    // With placeholder creds the tests fail-soft — we assert the health signal
93	    // *appears* (testing → unreachable), not that connectivity succeeds. The
94	    // settled failure badge reads "unreachable" (HealthBadge, Connections.tsx);
```

```
import { defineConfig, devices } from '@playwright/test';

// Browser E2E — drives the *real* app in a browser (dev-bypass auth, the seeded
// demo dataset), the missing other half of the full-stack smoke (#128). The
// httpx API smoke (backend/scripts/e2e_smoke.py) proves HTTP→service→DB; this
// proves the React app a user actually clicks.
//
// It assumes a running stack reachable at E2E_BASE_URL (default the compose
// frontend on :3000, whose Vite proxy forwards /api → the api service). Bring it
// up first: `docker compose up` + `python -m backend.scripts.seed_dev`.
//
// Locally the `webServer` block reuses that already-running :3000 dev server. In
// CI it starts its own `pnpm dev` (the backend is launched by the workflow step
// before Playwright runs). See frontend/e2e/README.md.
const baseURL = process.env.E2E_BASE_URL || 'http://localhost:3000';

export default defineConfig({
  testDir: './e2e',
  fullyParallel: true,
  // Fail the build if a `test.only` is committed; flaky-retry only in CI.
  forbidOnly: !!process.env.CI,
  retries: process.env.CI ? 2 : 0,
  workers: process.env.CI ? 1 : undefined,
  reporter: process.env.CI ? [['list'], ['html', { open: 'never' }]] : 'list',
  timeout: 30_000,
  expect: { timeout: 10_000 },
  use: {
    baseURL,
    trace: 'on-first-retry',
    screenshot: 'only-on-failure',
  },
  projects: [{ name: 'chromium', use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] } }],
  webServer: {
    command: 'pnpm dev --host --port 3000',
    url: baseURL,
    // Locally: reuse the compose/`pnpm dev` server already on :3000. In CI:
    // start a fresh one (the api is already up on :8000 from a prior step).
    reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI,
    timeout: 120_000,
    env: {
      VITE_AUTH_DEV_BYPASS: 'true',
      VITE_API_PROXY_TARGET: process.env.VITE_API_PROXY_TARGET || 'http://localhost:8000',
    },
  },
});
```

## Phase 4 shipped — PR #350 squash-merged into `main` (`55316b9`)

**Week 6 Phase 4 — drawer → page restructures** is complete and merged. All 14 CI checks green.

- **4.1** New Suite → `/suites/new` (shared `SuiteForm`; create continues to Add Check)
- **4.2** Edit flows → dedicated pages (`/connections/:id/edit`, `/suites/:id/edit`, `/suites/:id/checks/:id/edit`); **all three edit drawers removed**; dedups #204
- **4.3** New Connection categorized source-select (Orchestration-first + avatar/blurb/chevron cards)

**Along the way:**
- `/code-review` caught a real bug — edit pages didn't refetch on a param-only route change (could PATCH the wrong id). Fixed by keying each edit view on its route id, with a regression test; also parallelized CheckEdit's fetch.
- Two CI rounds flushed out the **Playwright e2e specs** (not part of `pnpm test`) still driving the old drawers — updated them to the page flows, plus a `Cancel`-locator strict-mode fix. The one vitest red in between was the unrelated flaky `LiveRunProgress` polling test, which passed on re-run.

**Docs:** `progress.md` + epic #175 synced — **Week 6 now 16/27**.

Remaining for Week 6: **Phase 5** (Profile content, Settings, Admin reconcile), **Phase 6** (error pages), and the **alerting track** (6 tasks: `ResultPublisher` seam, Teams notifications, suppression, dedup, adaptive card, severity routing). Want me to pick up Phase 5 next?

One thing worth noting for next time: the e2e specs live in `frontend/e2e/` and only run under Playwright (full stack), so `pnpm test` won't catch UI-flow breakage — I'll grep `frontend/e2e/` for affected flows up front on future UI refactors.

fontFamily: 'var(--font-mono)' }}>{s.target}</span>\n    </button>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction SuiteDetail({ s, go }) {\n  const { Card, DataTable, StatusBadge, Tag, Button } = DQs;\n  const [shareOpen, setShareOpen] = React.useState(false);\n  const ShareSuiteDrawer = window.ShareSuiteDrawer;\n  const cols = [\n    { key: 'name', header: 'Check', render: (v) => <strong style={{ color: 'var(--text-primary)', fontWeight: 600 }}>{v}</strong> },\n    { key: 'exp', header: 'Expectation', mono: true, render: (v) => <span style={{ color: 'var(--text-secondary)', fontSize: 12 }}>{v}</span> },\n    { key: 'kind', header: 'Kind', width: 110, render: (v) => <Tag tone=\"outline\">{v}</Tag> },\n    { key: 'status', header: 'Last', width: 110, render: (v) => <StatusBadge status={v} variant=\"soft\" /> },\n  ];\n  return (\n    <Card padding={0} style={{ overflow: 'hidden' }}>\n      <div style={{ display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'space-between', alignItems: 'flex-start', padding: '18px 20px', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--divider)' }}>\n        <div>\n          <div style={{ fontSize: 18, fontWeight: 600, color: 'var(--text-primary)' }}>{s.name}</div>\n          <div style={{ fontSize: 13, color: 'var(--text-muted)', marginTop: 4, fontFamily: 'var(--font-mono)' }}>{s.target}</div>\n        </div>\n        <div style={{ display: 'flex', gap: 8 }}>\n          <Button variant=\"ghost\" size=\"sm\" iconLeft={<window.Icon.share size={13} />} onClick={() => setShareOpen(true)}>Share</Button>\n          <Button variant=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\" onClick={() => go('suite-check', { suiteName: s.name })}>Add Check</Button>\n          <Button variant=\"primary\" size=\"sm\" iconLeft={<window.Icon.play size={13} />}>Run</Button>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n      <DataTable columns={cols} rows={s.checks} />\n      {ShareSuiteDrawer && <ShareSuiteDrawer open={shareOpen} onClose={() => setShareOpen(false)} suiteName={s.name} />}\n    </Card>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction SuitesScreen({ go }) {\n  const { Button } = DQs;\n  const [sel, setSel] = React.useState('s2');\n  const suite = SUITES.find((s) => s.id === sel) || SUITES[0];\n  return (\n    <div style={{ maxWidth: 1200, margin: '0 auto' }}>\n      <div style={{ display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'space-between', alignItems: 'center', marginBottom: 20 }}>\n        <div>\n          <h1 style={{ margin: 0, fontSize: 24, fontWeight: 600, color: 'var(--text-primary)' }}>Suites</h1>\n          <div style={{ fontSize: 14, color: 'var(--text-secondary)', marginTop: 4 }}>42 suites · <span style={{ color: 'var(--status-pass)', fontWeight: 600 }}>38 active</span> · <span style={{ color: 'var(--status-fail)', fontWeight: 600 }}>4 failing</span></div>\n        </div>\n        <Button variant=\"primary\" iconLeft={<window.Icon.plus size={15} />} onClick={() => go('suite-new')}>New Suite</Button>\n      </div>\n      <div style={{ display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: '320px 1fr', gap: 16, alignItems: 'start' }}>\n        <DQs.Card padding={6}>\n          <div style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: 2 }}>\n            {SUITES.map((s) => <SuiteListItem key={s.id} s={s} active={s.id === sel} onClick={() => setSel(s.id)} />)}\n          </div>\n        </DQs.Card>\n        <SuiteDetail s={suite} go={go} />\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nObject.assign(window, { SuitesScreen });\n","contentType":"application/javascript","isBase64":false,"truncated":false}

I have the full picture. The prototype convention is clear: **every screen is wrapped in `maxWidth + margin: '0 auto'`** (lists at 1200, forms at 720, source-picker at 880) — our pages set a `maxWidth` but flush-left, so they're not centered. And the prototype cards are a **taller vertical format in one even grid**, while ours are compact rows fragmented into per-type sub-rows.

Let me check the theme tokens and global card styles before implementing, and set up a branch.

```
=== styles.css interactive ===
/* Interactive cards (e.g. a connection) lift and deepen their shadow on hover,
   with a short ease so it feels responsive, not animated-for-its-own-sake. */
.dq-card--interactive {
  transition:
    box-shadow 0.18s ease,
    transform 0.18s ease;
}
.dq-card--interactive:hover {
  transform: translateY(-2px);
  box-shadow: 0 8px 20px rgba(16, 24, 40, 0.1);
}

/* Respect users who prefer reduced motion — keep the elevation, drop the move. */
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .dq-card--interactive {
    transition: box-shadow 0.18s ease;
  }
  .dq-card--interactive:hover {
    transform: none;
  }
}

/* Clickable suite list rows get a hover tint so they read as interactive (the
   card grid lifts; the rows can't lift, so a subtle background does the job). */
.dq-suite-row {
  transition: background 0.15s ease;
}
.dq-suite-row:hover {
  background: rgba(79, 70, 229, 0.04);
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/frontend/src/components/layout/Page.tsx",
  "content": "import { Flex } from 'antd';\nimport type { ReactNode } from 'react';\n\n/**\n * Per-screen content-column widths, mirroring the prototype (ADR 0022): list /\n * dashboard screens fill a wide column, authoring forms sit in a narrow column,\n * and the connection source picker is in between. Single source so screens don't\n * each hard-code a maxWidth.\n */\nexport const PAGE_WIDTH = { wide: 1200, picker: 880, form: 720 } as const;\n\n/**\n * Centered page content column. The prototype wraps every screen in\n * `maxWidth + margin: 0 auto`, so content is centred in the canvas instead of\n * hugging the sider on wide displays. This component is the one place that\n * contract lives — pages use it as their root rather than each re-deriving the\n * `marginInline: 'auto'` dance.\n */\nexport function Page({\n  width = PAGE_WIDTH.wide,\n  gap = 24,\n  children,\n}: {\n  width?: number;\n  /** Vertical gap between the page's stacked sections (header, body, …). */\n  gap?: number;\n  children: ReactNode;\n}) {\n  return (\n    <Flex vertical gap={gap} style={{ width: '100%', maxWidth: width, marginInline: 'auto' }}>\n      {children}\n    </Flex>\n  );\n}\n"
}
```

```
=== Home ===
import { Alert, Card, Descriptions, Spin, Typography } from 'antd';

import { useMe } from '../auth/useMe';

export function Home() {
  // Reuse the shared /me fetch (MeProvider) rather than fetching again — it's
  // already resolved by the time the Profile page renders.
  const state = useMe();

  if (state.status === 'loading') {
    return <Spin tip="Loading /api/v1/me…" size="large" style={{ marginTop: 80 }} />;
  }
  if (state.status === 'error') {
    return (
      <Alert
        type="error"
        showIcon
        title="Failed to load /api/v1/me"
        description={state.error}
        style={{ margin: 24 }}
      />
    );
  }
  return (
    <Card title="Authenticated as">
      <Descriptions column={1} bordered size="small">
        <Descriptions.Item label="Display name">{state.data.display_name ?? '—'}</Descriptions.Item>
        <Descriptions.Item label="Email">{state.data.email}</Descriptions.Item>
        <Descriptions.Item label="AAD object id">
          <Typography.Text code copyable>
            {state.data.aad_object_id}
          </Typography.Text>
        </Descriptions.Item>
        <Descriptions.Item label="Last seen">{state.data.last_seen_at ?? '—'}</Descriptions.Item>
      </Descriptions>
    </Card>
  );
}
```

```
63	          run={state.data.run}
64	          suiteName={state.data.suiteName}
65	          checks={state.data.checks}
66	        />
67	      )}
68	    </Flex>
69	  );
70	}
71	
72	function RunDetailBody({
73	  run,
74	  suiteName,
75	  checks,
76	}: {
77	  run: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof getRun>>;
78	  suiteName: string | null;
79	  checks: Check[];
80	}) {
81	  const checksById = useMemo(() => {
82	    const map = new Map<string, Check>();
83	    for (const c of checks) map.set(c.id, c);
84	    return map;
85	  }, [checks]);
86	
87	  // "Checks passed" counts only evaluated (severity-tier) results — skip/error
88	  // didn't evaluate a severity, so they're excluded from the denominator, same
89	  // as the ADR-0005 health score (a run with skipped checks shouldn't read worse
90	  // than its health).
91	  const evaluated = run.results.filter((r) => SEVERITY_STATUSES.has(r.status));
92	  const passed = evaluated.filter((r) => r.status === 'pass').length;
93	
94	  return (
95	    <Flex vertical gap={16}>
96	      <Flex justify="space-between" align="center" gap={12} wrap>
97	        <Typography.Title level={3} style={{ margin: 0 }}>
98	          {suiteName ?? `Run ${run.suite_id.slice(0, 8)}`}
99	        </Typography.Title>
100	        <DownloadMenu run={run} suiteName={suiteName} checks={checksById} />
101	      </Flex>
```

```
53	function groupByType(connections: Connection[]): [ConnectionType, Connection[]][] {
54	  const byType = new Map<ConnectionType, Connection[]>();

57	    if (group) group.push(c);
58	    else byType.set(c.type, [c]);

60	  return CONNECTION_TYPES.filter((type) => byType.has(type)).map((type) => [
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/frontend/src/components/layout/Page.tsx",
  "content": "import { Flex } from 'antd';\nimport type { ReactNode } from 'react';\n\n/**\n * Per-screen content-column widths, mirroring the prototype (ADR 0022): list /\n * dashboard screens fill a wide column, authoring forms sit in a narrow column,\n * and the connection source picker is in between.\n */\nconst WIDTHS = { wide: 1200, picker: 880, form: 720 } as const;\ntype PageWidthName = keyof typeof WIDTHS;\n\n/**\n * Centered page content column. The prototype wraps every screen in\n * `maxWidth + margin: 0 auto`, so content is centred in the canvas instead of\n * hugging the sider on wide displays. This component is the one place that\n * contract lives — pages use it as their root rather than each re-deriving the\n * `marginInline: 'auto'` dance. `width` takes a named preset (`wide` default,\n * `form`, `picker`) or a raw pixel number for one-off columns.\n */\nexport function Page({\n  width = 'wide',\n  gap = 24,\n  children,\n}: {\n  width?: PageWidthName | number;\n  /** Vertical gap between the page's stacked sections (header, body, …). */\n  gap?: number;\n  children: ReactNode;\n}) {\n  const maxWidth = typeof width === 'number' ? width : WIDTHS[width];\n  return (\n    <Flex vertical gap={gap} style={{ width: '100%', maxWidth, marginInline: 'auto' }}>\n      {children}\n    </Flex>\n  );\n}\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Yes — the changes are on disk (diff above confirms it), but they're genuinely subtle on the Suites page, for two reasons:

1. **Centering is invisible on a normal-width screen.** `Page` caps content at 1200px. Your content area is roughly `viewport − 220px sider − 48px padding`. On a ~1440px laptop that's ~1172px, already under 1200 — so the centering only visibly kicks in on wide (≥~1500px) monitors. Connections looked changed because I *also* restructured its grid; Suites only got centering + a small card bump.

2. **I only touched the Suites *browse grid*** (the card grid you see at `/suites` with nothing selected): `minmax(300→320)`, card padding `→20`, avatar `40→44`. Once you click a suite, you're in the master-detail (320px list + detail panel) which I only centered. And the prototype's Suites screen isn't a card grid at all — it's a **320px list + a detail card with a checks DataTable**, shown two-panel by default.

So the Suites page didn't visibly move much because it was already an even grid, and the prototype's Suites is structurally a list, not cards.

I held off committing (you rejected it). Before I commit, how far do you want the Suites page taken?

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "      {suites.map((suite) => {\n        const conn = connections.find((c) => c.id === suite.connection_id);\n        return (\n          <Card\n            key={suite.id}\n            size=\"small\"\n            className=\"dq-card--interactive\"\n            style={{ cursor: 'pointer' }}\n            styles={{ body: { padding: 20 } }}\n            onClick={() => onSelect(suite.id)}\n          >\n            <SuiteIdentity suite={suite} conn={conn} size={44} showDescription />\n          </Card>\n        );\n      })}\n    </div>\n  );\n}",
  "new_string": "      {suites.map((suite) => (\n        <SuiteBrowseCard\n          key={suite.id}\n          suite={suite}\n          conn={connections.find((c) => c.id === suite.connection_id)}\n          onSelect={() => onSelect(suite.id)}\n        />\n      ))}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * A suite's browse-grid card — the vertical layout the Connections cards use\n * (avatar top-left + env tag top-right, then name, connection · type, and an\n * optional 2-line description) so the two list pages read the same on first open.\n */\nfunction SuiteBrowseCard({\n  suite,\n  conn,\n  onSelect,\n}: {\n  suite: Suite;\n  conn: Connection | undefined;\n  onSelect: () => void;\n}) {\n  return (\n    <Card\n      className=\"dq-card--interactive\"\n      style={{ cursor: 'pointer' }}\n      styles={{ body: { padding: 20 } }}\n      onClick={onSelect}\n    >\n      <Flex vertical gap={14}>\n        <Flex justify=\"space-between\" align=\"flex-start\" style={{ minHeight: 44 }}>\n          {conn ? <ConnectionTypeAvatar type={conn.type} size={44} /> : <span />}\n          {conn && (\n            <Tag color={ENV_COLORS[conn.env]} style={{ marginInlineEnd: 0 }}>\n              {envLabel(conn.env)}\n            </Tag>\n          )}\n        </Flex>\n        <Flex vertical gap={2} style={{ minWidth: 0 }}>\n          <Typography.Text strong ellipsis style={{ fontSize: 15 }}>\n            {suite.name}\n          </Typography.Text>\n          <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\" style={{ fontSize: 13 }} ellipsis>\n            {conn ? `${conn.name} · ${CONNECTION_TYPE_LABELS[conn.type]}` : 'No connection'}\n          </Typography.Text>\n        </Flex>\n        {suite.description && (\n          <Typography.Paragraph\n            type=\"secondary\"\n            ellipsis={{ rows: 2 }}\n            style={{ margin: 0, fontSize: 13 }}\n          >\n            {suite.description}\n          </Typography.Paragraph>\n        )}\n      </Flex>\n    </Card>\n  );\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

Done with the layout work — committed locally (`2497dd6`), all gates green (259 tests, typecheck, lint, build). Take a look in your running app; if it's right I'll push and open the PR.

**What changed**
- **Centering** — new shared `Page` wrapper centers every screen (`maxWidth + margin: 0 auto`), prototype widths (lists 1200 · forms 720 · source picker 880). Note: on a ~1440px laptop the list column is already <1200, so centering only visibly kicks in on wide monitors — that's expected, not a no-op.
- **Connections cards** — dropped the per-type sub-headings (the cause of the ragged rows); each kind section is now one even grid of taller cards.
- **Suites browse grid** (first open) — cards now use the same vertical layout as Connections; the two-panel master-detail (once a suite is selected) is untouched, as you asked.
- **New Connection source picker** — larger cards.

**Your other questions:**

**"DQ run" in Results → Pipeline runs** — No, it's *not* the provider's run id. It's the **DataQ suite run(s)** that pipeline execution triggered on success, correlated via `triggered_by` (the marker `provider:pipeline_or_dag_id:provider_run_id`). The tag is colored by the DQ run's status and clicks through to `/results/:runId`. The provider's own run id lives in the `pipeline_runs` row and is embedded inside that linkage marker — it's the *key*, not what the column shows.

**No "Test Connection" button on the New Connection page** — correct, and it's a backend gap, not just a missing button. Today the API only tests a **saved** connection (`POST /connections/{id}/test`, which reads the stored secret from the secret store). The prototype tests an *unsaved* draft. Wiring that button honestly needs a new endpoint (`POST /connections/test` taking `{type, config, secret}` → run the adapter probe without persisting). That's a small backend slice (the `ConnectionAdapter` already has a test method) plus a security note (a secret travels in the request body). Want me to build it? It'd be its own small PR.

**Results page top KPI cards (Pass Rate / Anomalies / Avg Duration)** — deliberately not built, per the **KPI-honesty** decision (Phase 2.2): the real KPI cards live on **`/dashboard`** (Data Integrity, Pass-rate, Total Runs, Active Connections), which ADR 0022 makes the page-home for them — the Results page intentionally stays focused on the runs/pipeline tables. The prototype's specific Results cards can't all be shown truthfully in v1: **"Open Anomalies"** has no backing query (anomaly monitors are reserved for v1.x, ADR 0012), and the trend deltas (`+1.2%`, `-14%`) aren't computed. I *can* add a KPI strip to `/results` with the genuinely-backed metrics (pass rate / total runs / avg duration over the window) if you want it duplicated there — just without the anomalies card and the deltas.

Want me to (a) push + open the PR for the layout work, (b) add the Test-Connection draft endpoint + button, (c) add the backed KPI strip to Results, or some combination?

* No "Test Connection" button on the New Connection page - file an issue as of now.
* Results page top KPI cards (Pass Rate / Anomalies / Avg Duration) — got it, no need to add duplication in the result page. But we can enrich the dashboard one if possible.

```
263	  const navigate = useNavigate();
264	  // Pipeline runs + the DQ runs they triggered, both auto-refreshed so a newly
265	  // triggered run shows up against its pipeline run without a manual reload.
266	  const { state, reload } = useAsyncData(() => listPipelineRuns({ limit: LIST_LIMIT }));
267	  const { state: runsState, reload: reloadRuns } = useAsyncData(() =>
268	    listRuns({ limit: LIST_LIMIT }),
269	  );
270	  const [provider, setProvider] = useState<'all' | 'adf' | 'airflow'>('all');
271	
272	  // Refresh both sources on the poll cadence; `reload` keeps the current rows
273	  // visible across the refetch (no flash back to the spinner).
274	  useEffect(() => {
275	    const id = setInterval(() => {
276	      reload();
277	      reloadRuns();
278	    }, pollMs);
279	    return () => clearInterval(id);
280	  }, [reload, reloadRuns, pollMs]);
281	
282	  // triggered_by marker → the DQ runs it spawned (one pipeline run can trigger
283	  // several, one per binding).
284	  const runsByMarker = useMemo(() => {
285	    const map = new Map<string, Run[]>();
286	    if (runsState.status !== 'ok') return map;
287	    for (const r of runsState.data) {
288	      if (!r.triggered_by) continue;
289	      const list = map.get(r.triggered_by);
290	      if (list) list.push(r);
291	      else map.set(r.triggered_by, [r]);
292	    }
```

> AGENT

All done — committed locally on `feature/w6-layout-prototype-fidelity` (2 commits since main), every gate green (259 tests, typecheck, lint, format). Here's the rundown of your requests:

**Done in this branch:**
- **Source-picker cards** → now the same vertical large-card layout (avatar + name + blurb) as the Connections/Suites cards, for consistency.
- **"DQ run" / provider run id** → you're right. The Pipeline runs tab is orchestration-monitoring, so I added a **Provider run** column (the provider's own `provider_run_id`, monospace + copyable — the handle you'd paste into ADF/Airflow to debug). I kept the **DQ run** column too, since they answer different questions: *provider run* = "which orchestration execution," *DQ run* = "which data-quality run it triggered" (click-through to `/results/:runId`). Both visible, no information lost.
- **Pipeline runs date filter** → added (All time / 24h / 7d / 30d), mirroring the Runs tab exactly (same shared `Filter` + window logic).
- **Run-detail KPI card width** → the stat-cards row is now an even full-width grid, so its right edge lines up with the results table below.

**Filed as issues (not built):**
- **#351** — Test Connection button on the New/Edit connection page (needs a backend draft-test endpoint; milestone Week 6).
- **#352** — Dashboard KPI enrichment (honest options: an **Avg Duration** card + real period-over-period deltas; explicitly *not* "Open Anomalies" since nothing backs it; milestone Week 6).

On your "viewpoints" question for the DQ-run column — the only real tension is table width (two id-ish columns), which is why I made provider-run a fixed 200px ellipsis+copyable cell. If you'd rather make the provider run id a **deep link** into ADF/Airflow (not just copyable), we'd need the workspace/factory URL per connection — worth a follow-up if you want it.

I've **held the push/PR** so you can eyeball everything in your running app first. Want me to push `feature/w6-layout-prototype-fidelity` and open the PR now, or are there more tweaks?

> DEVELOPER

For the add new connection Orchestration says - Add your orchestration provider first — DataQ integrates by watching its runs, then triggers suites on completion.
But this is not completely true as we have CRON schedule, for which we don't need orchestration, correct?

> AGENT

You're right — that copy is misleading. DataQ has cron scheduling (Week-5 `schedules` table + dispatcher) plus manual runs and trigger-bindings, so a suite can run **without any orchestration provider**. Orchestration (ADF/Airflow) is one *optional* trigger path — "Add your orchestration provider first" overstates it. The copy was lifted verbatim from the prototype, but accuracy should win over prototype copy here. Let me fix it.

You're correct, and it's fixed. The note now reads:

> **Optional** — connect Azure Data Factory or Airflow to watch their pipeline runs and trigger suites on completion. Suites can also run on a schedule or on demand without one.

That stops implying orchestration is a prerequisite — it's just one of the trigger paths alongside cron `schedules` and manual/trigger-binding runs. Committed (`5f87f7b`); the `adf` blurb ("Trigger & monitor pipeline runs") and `airflow` ("Monitor DAG runs") were already accurate, so I left those.

One thing I **didn't** change: the **ordering** still leads with Orchestration (kept from the prototype). I left ordering as-is since you only flagged the copy, but say the word and I'll flip it to datasources-first.

> AGENT

Done both. The New Connection source picker now reads:

- **Warehouses** — *Run checks directly against tables in a cloud data warehouse.* → Snowflake
- **Lakehouses** — *Validate lakehouse tables governed by Unity Catalog.* → Unity Catalog
- **Cloud Storage** — *Run checks on flat files (CSV / Parquet) in object storage.* → ADLS Gen2, AWS S3
- **Orchestration** *(last)* — *Optional — connect Azure Data Factory or Airflow … Suites can also run on a schedule or on demand without one.* → ADF, Airflow

Datasources lead (a suite always needs one); orchestration sits last as the optional path. Each category note describes *what you do* with that bucket, distinct from the per-card blurbs (*what each product is*) — so no redundancy. Committed (`a390d2f`); 259 tests green.

Notes on things I verified are correct (no finding): `Page.tsx` WIDTHS lookup + numeric branch yields no NaN/undefined (`RunDetail` passes `1000`, others use named presets); `CONNECTION_KINDS` ordering + `i > 0` divider preserved; the redesigned `ConnectionCard` still wires Test/Edit/Re-auth/Delete/health and keeps the `aria-label` on the actions dropdown; `groupByType`/`ConnectionTypeSection` fully removed with no remaining references; `PipelineRunsTab` date filter matches Runs-tab semantics exactly (`started_at ?? created_at`, same `isWithinWindowDays`) and composes with the provider filter; `provider_run_id` is a non-null string so the copyable column renders safely; `SuiteIdentity` still used in the detail row; no dangling `PAGE_WIDTH` import; picker correctly groups ADF/Airflow under "Orchestration" with optional framing (CLAUDE.md §4 respected — not treated as queryable datasources). Both findings are low-severity comment/dead-flexibility cleanups; no correctness or behavior-loss defects found.

I'll re-run the failed job to unblock, and file an issue for the flake (not silently ignore it).

```json
{
  "command": "gh run rerun 28005285263 --failed 2>&1 | tail -3; echo \"---filing flake issue---\"; gh issue create --milestone \"Backlog (post-v1 / testing)\" --title \"Flaky CI: LiveRunProgress 'polls until terminal' vitest test intermittently fails\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Symptom\n`tests/components/LiveRunProgress.test.tsx > LiveRunProgress > polls until the run is terminal, then stops and links to results` intermittently fails in CI (`Frontend — tests`) with:\n\n> TestingLibraryElementError: Unable to find an element with the text: succeeded\n\n258/259 pass; only this one flakes. It passes locally and on re-run, so it's **timing-dependent**, not a real regression. Observed across PRs #350 and #353 (re-running the job clears it).\n\n## Likely cause\nFake-timers + async polling: the component polls `getRunProgress` on a self-scheduling timeout until terminal. The test advances timers, but the terminal-status re-render likely races the assertion (a pending promise microtask hasn't flushed when `getByText('succeeded')` runs) — i.e. a missing `await`/`act`/`findBy` around the final poll resolution.\n\n## Fix direction\n- Use `await screen.findByText('succeeded')` (retrying) instead of `getByText`, and/or wrap the final timer advance in `act` + `await` the flush.\n- Make `pollMs` + the fake-timer advance deterministic so the terminal poll resolves before the assertion.\n\n## Notes\n- Recurring CI flake → blocks otherwise-green PRs and forces job re-runs. Not urgent-functional, but worth de-flaking. Not in the scope of the PR that surfaced it (layout polish).\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -2",
  "description": "Re-run failed job + file flaky-test issue"
}
```

```
=== #250 full body ===
## Summary
Add a **Download** action on the run-detail drawer (Results page) to export an executed suite run's results in **both JSON and CSV**.

## Scope
- **JSON** — the `RunDetail` shape already fetched by the drawer (run metadata + per-check results). Reuse `frontend/src/utils/download.ts` `downloadJson`.
- **CSV** — flatten results to one row per check: `check · expectation_type · status · metric_value · observed_value · expected_value` (+ run id / suite / started_at / status header rows). Add a small `toCsv` helper alongside `download.ts`.
- **UI** — a "Download ▾" split/menu button on the run-detail drawer header (JSON / CSV), mirroring the suite **Export** button pattern.

## Frontend-only — no backend change
The Results read API (`GET /runs/{id}`) already returns everything the export needs. Stick to exactly what the API exposes.

## Gated on the PII resolution ⚠️
This is intentionally **deferred until the sample-failing-rows / row-level PII redaction question is resolved** (ADR 0018; tracked in #226). `sample_failures` are deliberately withheld from the API today, so an export must **not** include them until #226 lands a redaction-aware surface. Build the export over the non-PII fields first; fold in (redacted) sample rows only once #226 defines how.

## Acceptance
- [ ] Download JSON of a run from the drawer (round-trips the RunDetail).
- [ ] Download CSV of a run (opens cleanly in a spreadsheet; one row per check).
- [ ] No `sample_failures` / raw row data in either export until #226 resolves.
- [ ] Vitest for the `toCsv` helper + a drawer download test.

Relates to #226 (sample-row redaction) and the Week-6 dashboard "export" bundle.

=== #346 full body ===
## Summary

Register the **Phase-4 page routes** in [`frontend/src/App.tsx`](../blob/main/frontend/src/App.tsx) as part of the drawer→page restructure ([WEEK6_TODO Phase 4](../blob/main/WEEK6_TODO.md)).

## Context

The Week-6 nav/shell restructure (Phase 0.2, `0c6a219`) deliberately **deferred** these route registrations rather than landing throwaway stubs:

> **Deferred to their phases (no throwaway stubs):** `/suites/new` → 4.1; `/connections/:id/edit`, `/suites/:id/edit`, `/suites/:id/checks/:checkId/edit` → 4.2 (land with their real pages)

This issue tracks registering them when their real pages land in Phase 4.

## Routes to register

- `/suites/new` → `SuiteFlow` create page (Phase 4.1)
- `/connections/:id/edit` → `ConnectionNew` prefilled (Phase 4.2)
- `/suites/:id/edit` → `SuiteFlow` prefilled (Phase 4.2)
- `/suites/:id/checks/:checkId/edit` → `CheckNew` prefilled (Phase 4.2)

## Acceptance

- Each route renders its real page (not a stub) and is deep-linkable + refreshable.
- The corresponding **edit drawers are removed** (`ConnectionDrawer` / `CheckDrawer` / `SuiteDrawer` edit branches); parents drop `editing`/`drawer` state for `navigate()`.
- Nav highlight still prefix-matches via `SELECTABLE_KEYS`.

Dedups with #204 (drawer/delete duplication cleanup).
```

> AGENT

Audit done. Here's the picture across **237 merged PRs**:

## Labels — not a PR convention here
Only **12 of 237** merged PRs carry any label, and those are just the auto-`dependencies` tag on some Dependabot PRs. **Issues** carry the labels in this repo; PRs essentially don't. So mass-labelling closed PRs would fight the established pattern — I'd recommend **not** labelling PRs (unless you want to adopt a new convention going forward).

## Milestones — one real gap
| Group | Total | Missing milestone |
|---|---|---|
| **Dependabot** dep-bumps | 40 | **40** (100% — consistent: dep bumps aren't roadmap work) |
| **Substantive** (feature/docs/chore) | 197 | **67** |

- **Dependabot:** uniformly unmilestoned — that's a consistent convention, not a defect. Leave as-is.
- **Substantive 67 missing:** these are the real inconsistency. Weeks 1–3 PRs were milestoned reliably; from ~Week 4 onward it got spotty. The 67 span **Week 4** (#178–#248), **Week 5** (#251–#330), and **Week 6** (#331–#355) — including recent ones like #333/#350/#353/#355 which are clearly Week 6 but unmilestoned (while #347, same week, has it). Each one's correct week is derivable from the progress.md ledger.

Note these are retroactive bookkeeping — the project tracks delivery via `progress.md` + the weekly epics (#173–#177), so PR milestones are somewhat vestigial. But if you want them correct, the 67 are fixable.

How far do you want me to take the fix?

> AGENT

The progress.md grep is too noisy to rely on alone (many NONE / multi-hits from cross-references). But combined with PR titles + the established convention (early-credit PRs are milestoned to their **feature** week, e.g. #189/#190 are Week 5 despite merging early), I can map all 67 confidently. Here's the mapping (36 → W4, 24 → W5, 7 → W6 = 67):

## Closed-PR audit — done

**Labels:** PR labelling isn't a convention in this repo — only 12/237 merged PRs carry any label (sporadic auto-`dependencies` on Dependabot). Labels live on **issues**. Left PRs untouched (mass-labelling would be noise against the established pattern).

**Milestones:** fixed the real gap — backfilled **67 substantive merged PRs** to their correct roadmap week (using feature-week, consistent with how early-credit PRs like #189/#190 were already milestoned):
- **36 → Week 4** (custom-SQL, admin, version-history, check-editor closeout + deps/docs)
- **24 → Week 5** (execution + scheduling block)
- **7 → Week 6** (#228 Results scaffold, #331/#332 ADRs, #333/#350/#353/#355)

Had to set Weeks 4–5 via the API by milestone number (#1/#2) since those milestones are **closed** and `gh pr edit --milestone` only resolves open ones.

**Dependabot (40 PRs):** left unmilestoned — that's consistent (every Dependabot PR across all weeks is unmilestoned; dep-bumps aren't roadmap work). Say the word if you'd rather I milestone those too.

```
10	When filing a GitHub issue in DataQ, **assign the proper milestone** — don't leave it blank. The user caught me filing #231 with no milestone.
11	
12	**Why:** the repo uses GitHub milestones as the week ledger (Week 4/5/6/7/8 + a "Backlog (post-v1 / testing)" milestone); blank-milestone issues fall out of week accounting (open/closed counts) and lose their roadmap home.
13	
14	**How to apply:** pass `--milestone "<title>"` to `gh issue create` (or `gh issue edit N --milestone …` to backfill). Pick by where the work lands: a cleanup deferred from the current week → that week's milestone; post-v1 / pure-testing follow-ups → "Backlog (post-v1 / testing)". List exact titles with `gh api repos/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/milestones`. Relates to [[feedback_review_before_merge]] and [[feedback_working_agreements]].
```

```json
{

  "content": "---\nname: feedback_issue_milestone\ndescription: \"GitHub hygiene in DataQ: every issue gets a label + milestone; every PR gets a milestone\"\nmetadata: \n  node_type: memory\n  type: feedback\n  originSessionId: 80ae0122-48e1-4446-85d1-a5f459a89af0\n---\n\nDataQ GitHub hygiene the user wants going forward (confirmed 2026-06-23):\n- **Every issue:** proper **label(s) + milestone** — never blank.\n- **Every PR:** proper **milestone** (labels are NOT a PR convention here — only ~12/237 merged PRs had any; issues carry labels, PRs don't, so don't mass-label PRs).\n\n**Why:** milestones are the week ledger (Week 1–8 + \"Backlog (post-v1 / testing)\"); blank-milestone items fall out of week accounting (open/closed counts) and lose their roadmap home. Labels classify issues (type + priority) for triage.\n\n**How to apply:**\n- Issues: `gh issue create --milestone \"<title>\" --label \"<type>,priority/PN\"`. Label taxonomy = a **type** (`bug`/`enhancement`/`refactor`/`test`/`documentation`/`security`/`ci`/`dependencies`) + a **priority** (`priority/P1|P2|P3`); epics just get `epic`. Pick the milestone by where the work lands (cleanup deferred from the current week → that week; post-v1 / pure-testing → \"Backlog (post-v1 / testing)\").\n- PRs: set the milestone to the **feature week** the work belongs to (matches the early-credit convention — e.g. a Week-5 feature merged during Week 4 is milestoned Week 5, not the calendar week).\n- **Gotcha:** completed weeks' milestones (Week 1–5) are **closed**, and `gh pr edit --milestone` only resolves *open* milestones → it silently fails. Set closed milestones via the API by number: `gh api repos/:owner/:repo/issues/<n> -X PATCH -F milestone=<num>` (a PR is an issue in the API). Get numbers incl. closed with `gh api \"repos/:owner/:repo/milestones?state=all&per_page=100\"`.\n- Dependabot PRs are intentionally left unmilestoned (consistent — dep-bumps aren't roadmap work).\n\nRelates to [[feedback_review_before_merge]] and [[feedback_working_agreements]].\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Memory updated. Going forward I'll treat this as standing hygiene:

- **Every issue** → proper label(s) (a type like `bug`/`enhancement`/`refactor`/`test`/`docs` + a `priority/PN`) **and** a milestone.
- **Every PR** → a milestone, set to the **feature week** the work belongs to (not necessarily the calendar week it merges — matching how early-credit PRs are tracked). Labels stay issue-only, per the repo convention.

I also recorded the operational gotcha so I don't trip on it again: completed weeks' milestones (Week 1–5) are **closed**, so `gh pr edit --milestone` silently fails on them — those need `gh api … -X PATCH -F milestone=<number>`.

So when I file issues or open PRs from here on, they'll come pre-tagged correctly without you having to ask.

```
2

9	> repeatable retail data environment exercising all three DataQ run paths + the ADF/Airflow trigger path.

18	## 0. Prerequisites (accounts + local tooling)
19	
20	- [ ] Azure account/subscription enabled; resource group decided
21	- [ ] Snowflake account enabled (account locator, region, warehouse, role noted — store creds locally only)
22	- [ ] Databricks workspace + Unity Catalog enabled; a SQL Warehouse running; PAT issued
23	- [ ] Local tooling: `terraform`, `az` CLI, `snowsql` (optional), Docker + docker-compose, Python env for generators
24	- [ ] Decide secret handling for the harness (local `.env`, **never** committed — mirrors the repo rule)

26	---
27	
28	## 1. Terraform — cloud infra bootstrap (external, not git-tracked)
29	
30	> Provisions the **datasources** (ADLS / Snowflake / UC). Airflow is **local Docker** (§3), so Terraform
31	> does NOT provision Airflow in the v1 profile. ADF is used in its managed cloud form.
32	
33	- [ ] `terraform/` skeleton in the harness root with backend + provider blocks (azurerm, snowflake, databricks)
34	- [ ] **ADLS Gen2**: storage account + container(s) for landing zone (e.g. `landing/`, `raw/`)
35	- [ ] **Snowflake**: database + schema(s) + warehouse + a least-privilege role/user for DataQ to read
36	- [ ] **Unity Catalog**: catalog + RAW/SILVER/GOLD schemas; grants for the DataQ PAT
37	- [ ] **ADF**: data factory instance (for Flow A's load pipelines)
38	- [ ] Outputs: connection coordinates DataQ's connection editor needs (account URLs, warehouse ids, paths)
39	- [ ] `terraform apply` clean; record outputs into the harness `.env` (local only)
40	- [ ] Teardown verified (`terraform destroy`) so the demo env is disposable
41	- [ ] **No Azure resource names leak into the DataQ repo** (ADR 0010/0013) — they live only here

45	## 2. Retail data model + mock-data generators (external harness)
46	
47	> Domains → entities to generate. Believable retail data so checks/profiling/severity/trends look real.
48	
49	- [ ] **Product** — style, colour, material, SKU
50	- [ ] **Inventory** — on-hand stock, planned
51	- [ ] **Location** — store, address, store operating hours
52	- [ ] **Sales** — placed, shipped, audited
53	- [ ] **Return / Cancellation** — return, cancellation, guest, platform
54	- [ ] **Logistics** — shipment, tracking, carrier
55	- [ ] **Guest / Customer** — guest details (name, address, …), survey data, chatbot/feedback
56	- [ ] **Cross-cutting** — on-sale/discount, promotion/award/coupon, tax rate, exchange rate, product price, misc
57	- [ ] Generator script (Python — Faker or similar) emitting CSV/Parquet to the ADLS landing zone
58	- [ ] **Seed some deliberate DQ issues** (nulls, dupes, FK orphans, stale dates, volume drops) so checks actually fail — proves severity tiers + alerting, not just green dashboards
59	- [ ] Parameterise volume + a `--seed` for reproducibility; document run command in harness README

62	
63	## 3. Reference flows (each exercises a distinct DataQ run path)
64	
65	### 3a. Flow A — load to warehouse  →  `SnowflakeCheckRunner` / `UnityCatalogCheckRunner`
66	
67	- [ ] File lands in ADLS landing zone (from §2 generator)
68	- [ ] **ADF pipeline** (or Airflow DAG) picks the file up and loads it into Snowflake / UC
69	- [ ] On success, the orchestrator hits DataQ's trigger path (ADF webhook / Airflow callback → trigger binding)
70	- [ ] DataQ suite runs against the loaded **warehouse table**; result row persists
71	- [ ] Verify in `/results`: run succeeded, severity derived, metric_value populated
72	
73	### 3b. Flow B — medallion  →  UC tables
74	
75	- [ ] File lands in **UC RAW** layer
76	- [ ] **Databricks notebook** (external, not git-tracked) runs: RAW → Silver → Gold transforms
77	- [ ] DataQ suites run against the UC Silver/Gold tables
78	- [ ] (Optional) notebook completion triggers the DataQ run via Airflow/ADF binding
79	- [ ] Verify results per layer
80	
81	### 3c. Flow C — flat-file in place  →  `FlatFileCheckRunner` + batch resolution
82	
83	- [ ] File lands in ADLS and **stays a flat file** (no warehouse load)
84	- [ ] DataQ flat-file connection + suite targets the path with a **batching regex**
85	- [ ] Run resolves the **latest** batch and checks it directly
86	- [ ] Verify batch resolution picks the right file + result persists

91
```

```
=== reserved monitor kinds (ADR 0012) ===
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:11:value-level — they are **freshness** (a table stopped loading) and **volume** (a
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:14:this the leap from "a GX runner" to "a DQ platform": freshness, volume,
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:15:schema-drift, and anomaly auto-monitors.
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:28:   A freshness monitor has no expectation_type; it has an interval and a
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:32:2. **Metrics only live in JSONB `observed_value`.** A freshness monitor measures
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:33:   "hours since last load = 26.5"; a volume monitor measures "row count =
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:35:   charts and v1.1 anomaly baselines. **You cannot aggregate a value buried in a
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:49:varies by *monitor kind*. A freshness monitor on Snowflake and a freshness
docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md:55:  constraint over `('expectation', 'freshness', 'volume', 'schema_drift',

=== reconciliation comparison kind (ADR 0014) ===
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:1:# ADR 0014 — Cross-dataset reconciliation as a `comparison` check kind (reuse FastAPI_DataComparison engine)
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:9:We evaluated folding the **FastAPI_DataComparison** tool (`github.com/TheurgicDuke771/FastAPI_DataComparison`, MIT, same author) into DataQ. FDC is a pandas-based **source-vs-target reconciliation** engine: row-level and column-level diff joined on primary keys, producing matched / mismatched / additional-in-source / additional-in-target buckets, across SQLite, CSV, PostgreSQL, MySQL, MSSQL, Oracle, and Snowflake.
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:11:Reconciliation answers a question Great Expectations (ADR 0003) structurally does not: **"does dataset A match dataset B?"** — migration validation, ETL output checks, "did the copy land correctly." It is **complementary, not redundant**: GX asserts rules over *one* dataset; reconciliation diffs *two*. That makes it a genuine capability add rather than an overlap.
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:17:2. **Only FDC's engine is reusable; its app is not.** FDC's connection store (SQLite `connections.db`, passwords hex-encoded plaintext), auth (bearer token, local-only `127.0.0.1`), UI (Jinja templates), and result storage (disk files) all conflict with DataQ's hardened equivalents (Postgres, `SecretStore`/Key Vault, Azure AD, React, `results` table). The pure diff engine — `data_comparison/{column_comparison,record_comparison,get_dataset}.py` — is the part worth porting.
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:19:3. **A comparison check references *two* connections** (source + target), which breaks DataQ's invariant that a check/suite binds to **one** connection. This is the one genuine design decision and is deferred to a future ADR (see Decision §5).
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:23:**Reconciliation enters DataQ as a new reserved `check.kind = 'comparison'` on the ADR-0012 monitor-kind seam — reserved now, built post-v1. We reuse FDC's diff engine, not its app.**
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:25:1. **Reserve `comparison` as a `check.kind` value.** Add it to the Week-3 `checks.kind` CHECK set defined in [ADR 0012](0012-monitor-kind-seam.md) §1, alongside `freshness / volume / schema_drift / anomaly`. v1 never emits it — constraint-valid, no producer or consumer — exactly the reserved-kind pattern 0012 established. This is the only v1 action: one enum value in a migration being written anyway.
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:31:4. **Build is post-v1 (v1.x), not v1 scope.** Reserving the kind keeps the door open at near-zero cost — the same "pay nothing during v1 to keep the option live" discipline as [ADR 0010](0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md) / [ADR 0013](0013-marketplace-distribution-and-anti-lock-in.md). No reconciliation code, UI, or runner ships in v1.
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:33:5. **The two-connection check model is deferred to a future ADR (0015, pending).** *How* a `comparison` check carries source + target connection refs — a dedicated kind-specific table, two columns on `checks`, or a small join — stresses the single-connection check/suite invariant and is the real design decision. It is written when the build starts, not now. **Reserving the kind does not make a comparison check buildable in v1's schema** — that is deliberate (reservation ≠ buildability).
docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md:38:- Adds reconciliation — a capability GX cannot provide — with no new subsystem, sidecar, or plugin loader; it rides the monitor-kind dispatch already being built.
```

> AGENT

Grounded answer: your entity list is **broad enough for v1's core testing** — the three run paths (Snowflake/UC/flat-file), GX expectations, the profiler, severity tiers, and trends all have plenty to bite on, and the key-consistency point you just added is the single most important one for join/FK checks.

The gaps that matter aren't *more entities* for realism — they're **data shaped to exercise DataQ's roadmap differentiators**: the reserved monitor kinds (`freshness | volume | schema_drift | anomaly`, [ADR 0012](docs/adr/0012-monitor-kind-seam.md)) and cross-dataset reconciliation (`comparison`, [ADR 0014](docs/adr/0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md)). ADR 0012's whole thesis is "most real incidents are freshness/volume, not value-level — that's the leap from a GX runner to a DQ platform." So I'd add a handful of things, mostly about **structure**, not breadth:

**High value (unlocks DataQ capabilities you'll want to demo):**
1. **Order *header* ↔ order *line* grain.** "Sales" is currently event-states; split it into an order header + one row per SKU line. Most real checks live at line grain (`qty > 0`, `unit_price`, `line_total = qty × unit_price`), it's where `sku_id` joins Product, and it gives you the canonical *intra*-dataset reconciliation `sum(line_total) = order_total`.
2. **Payments / transactions + a finance ledger.** This is the missing domain *and* the reconciliation testbed: `order_total ↔ payment_captured ↔ refund`. That's exactly the source-vs-target the reserved `comparison` kind diffs. (You have tax/FX, but not the tender/settlement side.)
3. **A timestamped, incrementally-loaded feed/snapshot table** (e.g. hourly orders feed or daily inventory snapshot with a `load_ts`). Your "different batch/data load" note gestures at this — make *one* table explicitly time-series so a **freshness** check ("last load < 1h"), a **volume** band ("rows within ±20%"), and an **anomaly** (volume drop) each have a real target.
4. **A schema-drift-able dataset** — one feed whose columns you perturb across loads (add/drop/rename, type change) to exercise the reserved `schema_drift` kind.
5. **Channel / marketplace as a first-class dimension** (online · store · marketplace). "platform" only shows up under returns today; it's a common DQ slice axis (null channel, orphan marketplace).

**Optional (domain realism, less DataQ-specific):**
- Supplier/Vendor + Purchase Orders (inbound → inventory replenishment; freshness on inbound feeds).
- Fulfillment node / DC / warehouse (Logistics has carrier+tracking; the fulfilling location is often a join target).
- Catalog hierarchy (category/department/brand) for orphan/FK checks.
- A calendar/date dimension for trend slicing (fiscal week/season).

One reframe to keep it from ballooning: the bar isn't a complete retail warehouse — it's that **each DataQ capability has at least one target**. You're already covered for value-level GX (everywhere), flat-file batch (Flow C), warehouse/UC (Flows A/B), and PII redaction (guest details → #226). Items 1–4 above are the ones that light up the *reserved* kinds + reconciliation, which v1 only reserves but you'll want shaped data ready for when they're built.

Want me to fold items 1–5 into §2 of [HARNESS_TODO.md](HARNESS_TODO.md) (as sub-bullets under the data model + a short "shape the data for the reserved monitor kinds" note), and leave the optional ones as a parenthetical?

> DEVELOPER

Among these which data do you think we can test for flat-file route?

> AGENT

Good question — it sharpens which datasets go down which route. The flat-file route's distinguishing feature isn't "reading a file," it's **batch resolution** (a regex that resolves the *latest* time-partitioned file, PR #144) over data that **lands periodically and stays a file**. So the right flat-file candidates are the **time-partitioned event/transaction feeds and snapshots** — not the master/dimension data.

## Strong flat-file candidates

| Dataset | Why it fits flat-file | What it exercises |
|---|---|---|
| **Logistics / tracking events** *(best fit)* | Event feed dropping in batches; `tracking_number` held across `Ready For Shipment → In Transit → Delivered` across loads (your consistency point) | Batch resolution → latest file · freshness ("last drop < 1h") · the cross-batch key-stability check |
| **Orders / Sales hourly feed** | Append-only, partitioned by hour/day (`orders_2026-06-23-14.parquet`) | Latest-batch pick · volume band (±20%) · anomaly (volume drop) · line-grain value checks |
| **Inventory daily snapshot** | One snapshot file per day — the canonical "resolve the latest snapshot" case | Batch resolution · freshness · volume · null/range on stock |
| **Payments / transaction exports** | Finance systems classically export daily flat files | File-vs-table **reconciliation** target · numeric/precision checks |
| **Survey / chatbot / feedback** | Human/system exports, text-heavy, often CSV/JSON | Profiler on free-text · **PII** (email/phone) for #226 · semi-structured (→ Parquet/UC too) |

**Format split to cover both readers:** CSV for the human/system exports (store POS, tracking events, feedback); **Parquet** for the high-volume orders/inventory feeds — Parquet also exercises the zero-copy Arrow read path (PR #140), so make at least one Parquet.

## What I'd *not* put on the flat-file route
Master/reference/dimension data — **Product/SKU, Location, Catalog hierarchy (category/dept/brand), Calendar, Customer master**. These are slowly-changing and don't arrive in time-partitioned batches, so batch resolution is meaningless on them. They belong as **warehouse/UC tables** (Flow A load, Flow B medallion) — which is also what makes them the natural join/FK targets for the consistency checks.

## Concrete pick for Flow C (§3c)
Use **logistics tracking events** as the primary batch-resolution demo (cleanest fit, and it directly exercises the `tracking_number`-across-batches consistency you flagged), plus a **daily inventory snapshot** for the "resolve the latest snapshot + freshness/volume" angle. One CSV (tracking events), one Parquet (inventory snapshot) → both formats and both batch shapes (event-append vs daily-snapshot) covered.

> AGENT

Here's how I'd allocate the rest across the two warehouse routes, keeping each platform playing to its strengths and the system-of-record clean (which matters for reconciliation later).

## Snowflake — Flow A (ADF load → table, SQL-heavy)
The **transactional + financial core**, because Snowflake is where custom-SQL checks (ADR 0019), line-grain expectations, and SQL reconciliation shine:
- **Orders** (header + line items) — flagship; `qty>0`, `line_total = qty×unit_price`, `sum(line_total)=order_total`
- **Payments / Finance** — the `order_total ↔ payment ↔ refund` reconciliation via custom-SQL
- **Returns / Cancellations** — joins back to Orders (FK-orphan checks)
- **Customer / Guest master** + **Product / SKU master** — the dimensions those facts join to (also the PII story on Customer)

## Unity Catalog — Flow A (simple load → UC table, *no* transform)
A couple of **governed reference/dimension** tables, specifically to prove the `UnityCatalogCheckRunner` path *without* the medallion machinery (so you've tested "UC as a plain warehouse target" separately from the pipeline):
- **Location / store** (+ operating hours)
- **Promotions / pricing / coupons** (the cross-cutting reference data)
- **Channel / Marketplace** dimension

UC's identity is the *governance layer*, so governed reference data is a natural fit here.

## Databricks medallion — Flow B (bronze → silver → gold on UC)
The data that genuinely **transforms and aggregates through layers** — the lakehouse/ML use case:
- **Feedback / survey / chatbot** → **bronze** raw JSON/text · **silver** cleaned + PII-handled · **gold** sentiment/NPS feature table (the "ML feature store" story; #226 redaction belongs at silver)
- **Sales → revenue marts** → **bronze** raw sales events · **silver** cleaned daily sales by store·channel · **gold** revenue/units rollups

## Why this split earns its keep (two bonuses)
1. **The medallion *gold* tables are the natural home for the reserved freshness/volume/anomaly monitors** (ADR 0012) — aggregates that should refresh daily, where "didn't load" / "volume dropped" are the real incidents.
2. It sets up a **cross-platform reconciliation** (ADR 0014 `comparison`): Snowflake `Orders.order_total` ↔ Databricks `gold.daily_revenue` *should tie out* — a perfect two-connection diff demo, and a realistic "did the lakehouse copy land correctly" scenario.

**One discipline to keep:** designate **one system-of-record per entity** — Snowflake Orders is the truth; the medallion `gold` revenue is a *derived* aggregate that must reconcile back. Don't let Sales be authoritatively defined in two places, or the reconciliation checks lose meaning (it'd be generation noise, the thing the key-consistency rule guards against).

Full route map for reference:

| Dataset | Route | Platform / layer |
|---|---|---|
| Logistics tracking events | Flat-file (C) | ADLS · **CSV** · batch-resolved |
| Inventory daily snapshot | Flat-file (C) | ADLS · **Parquet** · latest-snapshot |
| Orders (header+line), Payments, Returns, Customer, Product | Warehouse (A) | **Snowflake** |
| Location, Promotions/pricing, Channel | Warehouse (A) | **Unity Catalog** (plain load) |
| Feedback/survey/chatbot, Sales→revenue marts | Medallion (B) | **Databricks** bronze→silver→gold (UC) |

## Now — perf testing (let's align before I write it)

DataQ is GX + Celery + Postgres, so "perf" has a few distinct axes. Here's what I'd put in a **§6 Performance / scale testing** and the open calls I'd want your steer on:

**The axes worth measuring** (all ride §2's parameterizable volume — generate S/M/L/XL, measure, set baselines):
1. **Run scaling** *(the headline)* — GX run wall-clock vs **rows × #checks-per-suite**, per datasource (Snowflake / UC / flat-file CSV+Parquet). Where does it fall over, and is it the warehouse query or GX's in-memory batch? Also worker **memory peak** (GX pulls the batch in).
2. **Profiler latency** — directly validates the known **#327** N+1 (per-column top-values) on a **wide** table; quantifies the fix's payoff.
3. **Concurrency / throughput** — N suites running at once: Celery pool saturation, the 60s scheduler beat under many due schedules (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), run-now storms → queue wait time.
4. **DB growth** — `/results`, `/dashboard/summary`, `/runs` p95 latency as `results`/`runs` grow to 100k+ rows; ties to the retention-sweep index (#323).
5. **Flat-file batch resolution at scale** — resolving "latest" among a large landing zone (thousands of files) — the list+regex cost.

**Three decisions I need from you:**
- **Priority** — all five, or lead with run-scaling + DB-growth (the two most user-visible) and defer concurrency/landing-zone?
- **Targets** — do you have any SLA in mind (e.g. "10M-row table × 20 checks < N min", "`/results` p95 < 500ms at 100k results"), or should the first pass just **establish baselines** and flag regressions?
- **When / where** — milestone **Week 8** (testing) or **Backlog (post-v1)**? And should it also feed the **QA/QE agent (§4)** as a perf-smoke, or stay a standalone harness script?

My default if you don't want to deep-dive: lead with **run-scaling + profiler(#327) + DB-growth**, **baseline-first** (no hard SLAs yet), milestone **Backlog (post-v1 / testing)**, as a standalone harness script that the QA/QE agent can later invoke. Say the word and I'll write §6 to match — or adjust the dials.

```
1	# ADR 0021 — Live test/demo-data environment (retail model) lives outside the repo
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-06-21
5	- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771
6	- **Related:** ADR [0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md) (orchestration abstraction — the flows exercise it), [0010](0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md) / [0013](0013-marketplace-distribution-and-anti-lock-in.md) (Azure/Key Vault is one impl behind each seam; no Azure resource names in the repo), [0003](0003-gx-only-for-v1.md) (GX run paths the flows feed). Discharges the standing **"live warehouse/file run — deferred Week-1 smoke"** caveat carried in CLAUDE.md §13 and `docs/progress.md`.
7	
8	## Context
9	
10	Every DataQ run path — Snowflake (`SnowflakeCheckRunner`), flat-file (`FlatFileCheckRunner` + batch resolution), Unity Catalog (`UnityCatalogCheckRunner`) — is unit-tested against canned in-memory DataFrames with the **network seam stubbed** (real GX execution, mocked IO). That is deliberate and correct for CI, but it means no run has ever touched a live warehouse, live ADLS/S3 object, or live UC table. The roadmap has carried this as the *"deferred Week-1 smoke"* since Week 1.
11	
12	Closing it needs a **realistic, repeatable data environment**: real datasources with believable data, real orchestration picking the data up, and the three DataQ run paths executing against it end-to-end. The data should model a real domain (retail) so checks, profiling, severity tiers and trends look like production, not toy fixtures.
13	
14	The question this ADR settles is **what, if anything, of that environment belongs in the DataQ source repo.**
15	
16	## Decision
17	
18	**The test/demo-data environment is a harness that lives _outside_ the DataQ source repo.** None of the setup code is committed:
19	
20	- **Terraform** that provisions Azure + Snowflake infra once accounts are enabled — local / separate, **not git-tracked** (also keeps Azure resource names out of the repo, per ADR 0010/0013).
21	- **Mock-data generators** for the retail model and the seed data they produce — out of repo.
22	- **The Databricks RAW→Silver→Gold notebook** that processes landed files — out of repo.
23	
24	What **is** git-tracked is only documentation that points at the harness and records this decision: this ADR, plus the roadmap / `progress.md` pointers that finally give the deferred-smoke caveat an owner.
25	
26	**Retail data model** the harness seeds (domains → entities): Product (style, colour, material, SKU) · Inventory (on-hand, planned) · Location (store, address, store hours) · Sales (placed, shipped, audited) · Return/Cancellation (return, cancellation, guest, platform) · Logistics (shipment, tracking, carrier) · Guest/Customer (details, survey, chatbot feedback). Plus cross-cutting: on-sale/discount, promotion/award/coupon, tax rate, exchange rate, product price, misc.
27	
28	Data is split across **ADLS · Snowflake · Unity Catalog**; orchestration across **ADF · Airflow** (both behind the existing `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction — ADR 0004; no provider-specific branching).
29	
30	**Three reference flows** the harness drives, each exercising a distinct DataQ run path:
31	
32	- **Flow A — load to warehouse.** File lands in ADLS → ADF/Airflow picks it up → loads into Snowflake / UC → DataQ runs a suite against the warehouse table (`SnowflakeCheckRunner` / `UnityCatalogCheckRunner`).
33	- **Flow B — medallion.** File lands in UC RAW → Databricks notebook runs → processes RAW → Silver → Gold → DataQ runs suites against the UC tables.
34	- **Flow C — flat-file in place.** File lands in ADLS and **stays a flat file** (no warehouse load) → DataQ runs a suite directly against it (`FlatFileCheckRunner` + batch resolution picks the latest batch).
35	
36	**Hosting — local/fast-iteration profile (chosen).** Airflow runs **locally via the official `apache/airflow` docker-compose** (webserver + scheduler + Postgres metadata DB + optional Celery workers), while **ADLS / Snowflake / Unity Catalog point at real cloud accounts**. This keeps the orchestration loop cheap and fast to iterate while the datasources stay realistic. Terraform is **not** used for the local Airflow (docker-compose is the norm; the `kreuzwerker/docker` provider is unnecessary friction); ADF, being managed, is exercised in its cloud form. A fully-cloud profile (Terraform-provisioned **Azure Managed Airflow** or AKS-hosted, alongside the rest of the infra) is a valid alternative for a more production-like run, but is **not** the v1 default. Either way Airflow stays an `OrchestrationProvider` behind the abstraction (ADR 0004) — DataQ is agnostic to how it is hosted.
37	
38	## Consequences
39	
40	**Positive**
41	- The deferred live-warehouse/file smoke gets a concrete, owned mechanism instead of floating as a caveat.
42	- The repo stays clean of infra/seed/harness code and Azure-specific resource names (ADR 0010/0013 anti-lock-in holds).
43	- The three flows give real end-to-end coverage of all three run paths **and** the ADF + Airflow orchestration trigger-on-success path (ADR 0004), against believable retail data.
44	- Profiler, severity tiers, and Week-6 trends/anomaly baselines get realistic data to render against.
45	
46	**Negative / watch**
47	- The harness is unversioned alongside the product; its setup steps must be documented well enough to reproduce (README in the external harness location).
48	- **Boundary to hold:** if any harness artifact later becomes a *generic, user-facing reference template* (the way `integrations/airflow/dataq_airflow_callback.py` is a shipped template, **not** app code), it would move into `integrations/` (e.g. `integrations/databricks/`) and become git-tracked — but the retail-specific harness stays out. This ADR governs the demo harness, not future generic templates.
49	
50	## Alternatives considered
51	
52	- **Commit the harness into the repo (e.g. `scripts/mockdata/`, `infra/terraform/`).** Rejected: bloats the product repo with environment-specific seed + Azure resource names, working against the anti-lock-in posture (ADR 0010/0013), and couples product CI to harness churn. Credentials-in-tracked-files risk too (working agreement).
53	- **Keep relying on canned-frame unit tests only.** Rejected: never discharges the live smoke; profiling/trends keep running on toy data.
54	- **Ship the Databricks notebook as an `integrations/` template now.** Deferred: it's currently the retail-specific harness, not a generalised user template. Revisit if/when a generic medallion-integration reference is wanted.
55	
```

> AGENT

Read it. My recommendation: **no new ADR needed** — and here's the honest reasoning rather than just spinning one up.

**Why not:**
1. **ADR 0021 already owns this decision space** — it records the core call (harness lives outside the repo, retail model, three flows A/B/C, hosting profile). Everything we added to HARNESS_TODO today is **execution detail *within* that decision**, which 0021 itself says belongs outside the repo: *"What is git-tracked is only documentation that points at the harness and records this decision."*
2. **The genuinely-architectural bits already have ADRs.** Reserved-kind data shaping → **ADR 0012**. Cross-platform reconciliation (the Snowflake↔Databricks `comparison` demo) → **ADR 0014**. Data across ADLS/Snowflake/UC + ADF/Airflow → **0021 + 0004**. An ADR for the route map would just re-derive those.
3. **The rest is tactical and will churn.** Which dataset lands on which platform, the key-consistency generation strategy, the perf axes — these are working-plan choices that shift as you build the harness. ADRs are for durable decisions-with-rationale; routing test fixtures through one would dilute the log, and it'd drift out of date (the route map's natural home is the gitignored tracker, which is exactly where it is).

**The one real gap I'd flag:** ADR 0021's *committed* entity list is now **stale** vs the tracker — it still says "Sales (placed, shipped, audited)", with no Orders header/line split, no Payments/Finance, no Channel, no key-consistency or route map. A reader of the ADR gets an out-of-date picture.

So the proportionate fix isn't a new ADR — it's a **one-line pointer in ADR 0021** saying the *detailed, evolving* data model + dataset→run-path route map + reserved-kind shaping + system-of-record discipline + perf scope live in the harness tracker (keeping 0021 as the strategy-of-record, not a spec that drifts). That respects 0021's own "detail lives outside" principle without duplicating-then-rotting.

Want me to add that pointer to ADR 0021 (and lightly note the perf-testing scope in its Consequences)? That's the right amount of committed record here — a full new ADR would be over-documentation.
